The Rust downloader preserves the narrow network boundary: refuses elevated execution, accepts only credential-free HTTPS URLs, rejects fragments, disables redirects with `WINHTTP_OPTION_REDIRECT_POLICY_NEVER`, enforces signed-metadata vs package size budgets, writes `.download` partial files, verifies SHA-256 for package downloads using Rust package-core, publishes with `MoveFileExW`, and keeps the product binary name through `tools/build-rust-downloader-cli.ps1`. Source contract now fails if the old C++ downloader source returns. Passed: PowerShell parser check for `tools/build-rust-downloader-cli.ps1`; `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; x64 `cargo test --locked -p fcitx5-package-core --bin fcitx5-downloader --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` with 5 tests; x64 `cargo clippy --locked -p fcitx5-package-core --bin fcitx5-downloader --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -- -D warnings`; x64 CMake build `fcitx5_downloader`, `fcitx5_control_package_integration_test`, `fcitx5_source_contract_test`; x64 CTest `downloader-version`, `downloader-boundary-smoke`, `control-package-stopped-service-contract`, `source-contract`; x86 CMake build of the same targets; x86 CTest of the same tests; x86 `cargo test --locked -p fcitx5-package-core --bin fcitx5-downloader --target i686-pc-windows-msvc` with 5 tests; x64/x86 `tools/check-runtime-security.ps1`; `git diff --check`. | This completes the downloader Rust cutover while preserving the downloader as the only package component allowed to import WinHTTP. It does not complete the larger non-Engine Rust objective: updater, deployer, control/launcher shells, shared protocol/IPC/platform/common, Config shipping UI, Candidate UI, and TSF shipping cutover still require separate gated slices and real-host/differential evidence where applicable. |
